A\nsystem of multiple parameters for proper performance assessment, promotion and\nsalary increase were implemented to the faculty members.\nResearch Activities\nThe Research and Development Cell (RDC) of the University acts as the central hub for\nplanning and execution of various strategies to enhance research and consultancy. This\nencompasses diverse activities, including setting up new research facilities, partnering with\nvarious funding organizations, enabling technology transfers, handling Intellectual Property\nRights (IPR), supervising Ph.D. programme, and cultivating collaborations with industry partners\nto explore joint research and consultancy ventures.\nGITAM has witnessed impeccable growth in the R&D activities during the last two years. The\nestablishment of the Multidisciplinary Unit of Research on Translational Initiatives (MURTI) at\nVisakhapatnam and Bengaluru campuses, which stand as a beacon of interdisciplinary\nresearch & innovation and acts as a catalyst for change in the University’s research culture.\nThe objective of MURTI is to create cross-disciplinary research hubs that are engaged in goal-\noriented and time-bound translational initiatives. As part of this initiative, state-of-the-art\nlaboratories tailored to specific campus requirements are being established, research clusters\nare being formed, and expert resources are being recruited to empower faculty members to\nconduct path-breaking research.\nResearch Publications:\nThe faculty members at GITAM have published a total of 2,042 research papers during the\nyear 2023-24 as compared to 1932 in 2022-23 as shown below:\nSl.\nNo.\n\nCampus / School / Institute\nNo. of\npapers\npublished\n2021-22\nNo. of papers\npublished\n2022-23\nNo. of\npapers\npublished\n2023-24\nVisakhapatnam Campus\n GITAM School of Technology 455 509 437\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n GITAM School of Science\n GITAM School of Business\n GITAM School of Pharmacy\n GITAM Institute of Medical\nSciences and Research\n GITAM School of Humanities and\nSocial Sciences\n GITAM School of Architecture\n Centre for Distance Learning\n GITAM School of Law\n GITAM School of Nursing\n GITAM School of Physiotherapy\nHyderabad Campus\n GITAM School of Technology\n GITAM School of Science\n GITAM School of Business\n GITAM School of Pharmacy\n GITAM School of Humanities and\nSocial Sciences\n Kautilya School of Public Policy\nSl.\nNo.\n\nCampus / School / Institute\nBengaluru Campus\n GITAM School of Technology\n GITAM School of Science\n GITAM School of Business\n GITAM School of Humanities and\nSocial Sciences\n\n\uf0b7 The percentage of Indexed\nScience, PubMed, IEEE and Google Scholar) has constantly breached the 94% mark with a\nhigh of 77% in AY 2023-2024, preceded by 72% in AY 2022\n2022\\.\n\n\n\nPage 17\nGITAM School of Science 295 382\nGITAM School of Business 88 80\nGITAM School of Pharmacy 128 124\nGITAM Institute of Medical\nSciences and Research\n54 46\nGITAM School of Humanities and 18 10\nGITAM School of Architecture 10 2\nCentre for Distance Learning 2 2\nGITAM School of Law 3 3\nGITAM School of Nursing - As on today, over 950 Ph.D. theses awarded by GITAM have been deposited in\nShodganga and made available to the entire scholarly community in open access.\n\n\uf0b7 The e-resources subscribed by the library can be accessed through the intranet and\nthe internet. The winners of the SmartIDEAthon participated\nin an “International-Startup Pitch Spring”, a two-week immersion program held at\nNortheastern University, Boston, USA.\nPlacement\n\uf0b7 GITAM Career Guidance Centre (GCGC) of the University assists students in exploring\ntheir career choices, including higher studies in India/abroad, research & innovation,\ncompetitive exams, including UPSC & GATE and campus placements by providing\ncareer counseling& mentoring through trained faculty and also providing training to the\nstudents. GITAM’s high standards are well-recognized by multinational corporations in\nIT, Service Sector, Core Industry, Healthcare, FMCG, BFSI, Retail, Consultancy and\nother fields across the country, drawing them to conduct campus interviews on a large\nscale.\n\n\uf0b7 The year 2023-24 was challenging with respect to placements as many 32orporate\nwere consolidating their employee base, especially hired during the pandemic and\nhence, the recruitments have slowed down. During this year, over 1800 students got\nplaced (63%) with multiple offers of around 2,455 through the campus placement\ndrives conducted across the three campuses by different MNCs from across India.\nPlacements are still under progress and the final placement percentage is expected to\nbe around 80%.\nVisakhapatnam Campus:\nThe placement activity at the Visakhapatnam is still under progress, with a large number of\nIndian companies and MNCs visiting the campus for placement drives, and a few more lined\nup.\n\uf0b7 GITAM School of Technology: Five hundred and ten students received more than 600\noffers from different MNCs across India. REPORT\nTo\nThe Management of GITAM (Deemed to be University) of GANDHI INSTITUTE OF TECHNOLOGY AND MANAGEMENT (REGD\nNo: 277 of 1980), VISAKHAPATNAM.\nOpinion\nWe have audited the accompanying financial statements of GITAM (Deemed to be University) of GANDHI INSTITUTE OF TECH-\nNOLOGY AND MANAGEMENT (REGD No. 277 of 1980) VISAKHAPATNAM, which comprise the Balance Sheet as at 31\nst\nMarch\n2024, the Income and Expenditure account and cash flow statement for the year then ended and notes to financial statements, in-\ncluding a summary of significant accounting policies and other explanatory information.\nIn our opinion and to the best of our information and according to the explanations given to us, the aforesaid financial statements\ngive a true and fair view in conformity with the accounting principles generally accepted in India, of the state of affairs of the GITAM\n(Deemed to be University) as at March 31, 2024 and its Surplus and cash flows for the year ended on that date.\nBasis for Opinion\nWe conducted our audit in accordance with the Standards on Auditing (SAs) issued by Institute of Chartered Accountants of India. Our\nresponsibilities under those standards are further described in the Auditor’s Responsibilities for the Audit of the Financial Statements\nsection of our report. We are independent of the entity in accordance with the Code of Ethics issued by ICAI and we have fulfilled our\nother ethical responsibilities in accordance with the Code of Ethics. We believe that the audit evidence we have obtained is sufficient\nand appropriate to provide a basis for our opinion.\nResponsibilities of Management and Those Charged with Governance for the Financial Statements\nThe Management is responsible for the preparation and presentation of these financial statements in accordance with generally ac-\ncepted accounting principles including the Accounting Standards issued by Institute of Chartered Accountants of India, and for such\ninternal control as management determines is necessary to enable the preparation of financial statements that are free from material\nmisstatement, whether due to fraud or error.\nIn preparing the financial statements, management is responsible for assessing the entity’s ability to continue as a going concern, dis-\nclosing, as applicable, matters related to going concern and using the going concern basis of accounting unless management either\nintends to liquidate the entity or to cease operations, or has no realistic alternative but to do so.\nThe Management is responsible for overseeing the entity’s financial reporting process.\nAuditor’s Responsibilities for the Audit of the Financial Statements:\nOur objectives are to obtain reasonable assurance about whether the financial statements as a whole are free from material mis-\nstatement, whether due to fraud or error, and to issue an auditor’s report that includes our opinion. Reasonable assurance is a high\nlevel of assurance, but is not a guarantee that an audit conducted in accordance with SAs will always detect a material misstatement\nwhen it exists. Misstatements can arise from fraud or error and are considered material if, individually or in the aggregate, they could\nreasonably be expected to influence the economic decisions of users taken on the basis of these financial statements.\nAs part of an audit in accordance with SAs, we exercise professional judgement and maintain professional skepticism throughout the\naudit. We also:\n • Identify and assess the risks of material misstatement of the financial statements, whether due to fraud or error, design and\nperform audit procedures responsive to those risks, and obtain audit evidence that is sufficient and appropriate to provide a\nbasis for our opinion. The risk of not detecting a material misstatement resulting from fraud is higher than for one resulting\nfrom error, as fraud may involve collusion, forgery, intentional omissions, misrepresentations, or the override of internal\ncontrol.\n • Obtain an understanding of internal control relevant to the audit in order to design audit procedures that are appropriate in\nthe circumstances, but not for the purpose of expressing an opinion on the effectiveness of the entity’s internal control.\n • Evaluate the appropriateness of accounting policies used and the reasonableness of accounting estimates and related\ndis- closures made by management.\n • Conclude on the appropriateness of management’s use of the going concern basis of accounting and, based on the audit\nevidence obtained, whether a material uncertainty exists related to events or conditions that may cast significant doubt on\nthe entity’s ability to continue as a going concern. If we conclude that a material uncertainty exists, we are required to draw\nattention in our auditor’s report to the related disclosures in the financial statements or, if such disclosures are inadequate,\nto modify our opinion. Our conclusions are based on the audit evidence obtained up to the date of our auditor’s report. How-\never, future events or conditions may cause the entity to cease to continue as a going concern.\n\n\n\n\n\n\nPage 8\n\n17\nth\n Annual Report 2023-24\n9%\n39%\n35%\n4%\n4%\n1%\n0%\n6%\n0%\n2%\nFaculty-wise distribution of PG Students\nEngineering -218\nScience -958\nBusiness - The financial statements are prepared under historical cost convention and on accrual basis in accordance with the Generally\nAccepted Accounting Principles in India (Indian GAAP) and accounting standards mandated by the Institute of Chartered\nAccountants of India.\n2\\. Stocks are valued at cost or Net Realisable value whichever is lower.\n3\\. Property, Plant and Equipment: Property, Plant and Equipment are stated at cost less accumulated depreciation under\nhistorical cost convention.\n4\\. Depreciation rates have been revised effective from this financial year. Depreciation is provided under Written down value\nmethod at the following rates. In respect of assets that are used for less than six months, depreciation is provided at half of the\nrates\n Sl No Name of the Asset Rate\n 1 Buildings – Academic & Others 10%\n 2 Buildings – Residential 5%\n 3 Roads & Drains 10%\n 4 Water Works 10%\n 5 General Equipment 15%\n 6 Laboratory Equipment 15%\n 7 Office Equipment 15%\n 8 Computers 40%\n 9 Vehicles 15%\n 10 Telecom Equipment 15%\n 11 Internet Equipment 40%\n 12 Furniture 10%\n 13 Sports & Gym Equipment 15%\n 14 Solar Power System 40%\n 15 Library Books 40%\n 16 Software / Intangible 40%\n 17 Electrically Operated Vehicles including battery powered or\n fuel-cell powered vehicles 40%\n 18 Water Pollution Control Equipment 40%\n 19 Life Saving Medical Equipment 40%\n5\\. Donations Received and Hostel admission receipts are treated as Capital and accordingly added to Capital Fund in the\nBalance Sheet. The Interest received on Earmarked Funds is added to the concerned fund account to meet the expenditure\nfor the specific purpose.\n6\\. Interest on borrowings availed from scheduled banks utilized for acquisition of property, plant and equipment is capitalized on\npro rata basis up to the date of putting to use of the related asset.\n7\\. Revenue recognition: Tuition and other fees are recognized as income on monthly pro-rata basis.\n8\\. Grants received from various granting agencies and related expenditure are accounted for to the extent utilized. Unspent\nbalances are shown as current liability under the head “grants received in advance”.\n9\\. Investments are shown at cost of acquisition.\n\n\n\n\n\n\nPage 8\n\n17\nth\n Annual Report 2023-24\n9%\n39%\n35%\n4%\n4%\n1%\n0%\n6%\n0%\n2%\nFaculty-wise distribution of PG Students\nEngineering -218\nScience -958\nBusiness - 872\nPharmacy - 107\nHumanities - 102\nLaw -17\nArchitecture -9\nMedical Sciences -154\nNursing - 3\nPublic Policy - 56\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\uf0b7 Following Table gives the details regarding admissions in different courses offered by\nthe Schools /Institutes of all the three Campuses for the Academic Year 2023-24 as\ncompared to 2022-23 and 2021-22.\n\nSchool /\nInstitute\n2021-22 2022-23 2023-24\nUG PG Total UG PG Total UG PG Total\n\nVisakhapatnam Campus\n\nGITAM School of\nTechnology\n1751 84 1835 1705 67 1772 1814 84 1898\nGITAM School of\nBusiness\n391 301 692 334 240 574 245 289 534\nGITA School of\nHumanities and\nSocial Sciences\n67\n 46 113 50 33 83 65 37 102\nGITAM School of\nScience\n607 530 1137 314 426 740 319 344 663\nGITAM School of\nPharmacy\n100 42 142 103 44 147 88 46 134\nGITAM School of\nLaw\n110 21 131 90 22 112 88 17 105\nGITAM School of\nArchitecture\n24 20 44 30 6 36 31 4 35\nGITAM Institute\nof Medical\nSciences\n&Research:\nMedical\n150\n 51 201 134 51 185 150 52 202\nPage 226\n97\nGITAM (Deemed to be University)\n10\\. Claims not acknowledged as debts:\n9.1 Dispute with M/s M.S.Ramayya Constructions (P) Ltd:\n M/s.M.S.Ramayya Constructions (P) Ltd.made a claim of Rs 8250 lakhs and moved the Hon’ble High Court seeking appointment\nof Arbitrator to adjudicate the dispute with M/s GITAM. Hon’ble High Court has granted interim direction to issue notice to the\nrespondent. The case is pending in the court.\n9.2 Dispute on Vizag Land:\n Greater Visakhapatnam Municipal corporation (GVMC) demolished part of the compound wall of GITAM Premises at Vizag.\nGITAM approached the Hon’ble High Court of Andhra Pradesh and court stayed any further demolition by GVMC.\n9.3 Deletion from the Sec -22A of the Registration Act:\n The GITAM land was erroneously categorized under Sec 22A of the Registration Act which was challenged by the GITAM\nunder WP no. 30106/2022 in the High Court of Andhra Pradesh. The High Court directed the authorities to delete the land from\nthe prohibited list. The matter is pending with the authorities to take action on the order of the High court.\n9.4 Dispute with TSSPDCL:\n Southern Power Distribution Company of Telangana State Limited (TSSPDCL) issued a “Notice under Link Service” to pay\nan amount of Rs 108,11,06,157. The demand was issued on GITAM as M/s VBC Ferro Alloys Limited failed to pay the dues\nto TSSPDCL. GITAM raised a dispute on that issue before Hon’ble High court of Telangana restraining the TSSPDCL from\ndisconnecting the power supply to GITAM. VBC Ferro Alloys Limited entered into a settlement agreement with and settling the\ndues to TSSPDCL in installments.\n9.5 Disputed Land at Hyderabad\n Vide sale deed No. 891/2008, GITAM purchased an extent of Ac 5.03 of land in Burugupally village from Mr M. Gyaneshwar\nS/o.Narsimhulu. However, the Hon\'ble High Court of Telangana in their combined orders in WA 711 of 2008 and WA 911\nof 2008 gave judgement setting aside the above sale deed. Based on the legal advice we have filed Special Leave Petition\nNo 11403/2023 with the Hon’ble Supreme Court of India. The petition was heard on 05-04-2023 and Hon’ble Supreme court\ndirected to maintain the status quo on the property. No provision is envisaged as no loss is projected at this juncture.\n As per our Report even date attached\n For SRIRAMAMURTHY & CO\n Chartered Accountants\n Sd/- Sd/- Sd/-\n D. GUNASEKARAN K.
